# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

Verified working 2026-09-11 for login, node/guest\nenumeration, vm config/status, and guest-agent exec dispatch/polling.\n\nUsage:\n  pve_api.py login\n  pve_api.py realms|nodes|resources|permissions\n  pve_api.py vmconfig <node> <vmid>\n  pve_api.py vmstatus <node> <vmid>\n  pve_api.py raw <METHOD> <path> [body-string]\n\nAlso importable for scripts:\n  from pve_api import api, load_creds\n  status, data = api(\"POST\", \"/api2/json/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec\",\n                     body={\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", \"echo <b64> | base64 -d | bash\"]})\n\"\"\"\nimport sys, json, ssl, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n\nBASE = \"https://10.0.20.100:8006\"\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context()\nCTX.check_hostname = False\nCTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\nCREDS = os.path.expanduser(\"~/.pve_ldap_bot\")\n\n\ndef load_creds():\n    with open(CREDS) as f:\n        lines = [l.rstrip(\"\\n\") for l in f if l.strip()]\n    return lines[0], lines[1]\n\n\ndef login():\n    user, pw = load_creds()\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({\"username\": user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\", \"password\": pw}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + \"/api2/json/access/ticket\", data=data)\n    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=10) as r:\n        d = json.load(r)[\"data\"]\n    return d[\"ticket\"], d[\"CSRFPreventionToken\"], user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\"\n\n\ndef api(method, path, body=None):\n    ticket, csrf, _ = login()\n    return raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body)\n\n\ndef raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body=None):\n    data = None\n    if isinstance(body, dict):\n        # doseq=True is CRITICAL: list-valued params (e.g. agent/exec \"command\"\n        # array) are silently DROPPED by plain urlencode -> the POST succeeds\n        # but the exec never runs.\n        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body, doseq=True).encode()\n    elif isinstance(body, str):\n        data = body.encode()\n    r = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data, method=method)\n    if ticket:\n        r.add_header(\"Cookie\", \"PVEAuthCookie=\" + ticket)\n    if csrf and method not in (\"GET\",):\n        r.add_header(\"CSRFPreventionToken\", csrf)\n    try:\n        with urllib.request.urlopen(r, context=CTX, timeout=20) as resp:\n            return resp.status, json.load(resp)\n    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n        try:\n            return e.code, json.load(e)\n        except Exception:\n            return e.code, {\"raw\": \"http error %d\" % e.code}\n\n\ndef main():\n    cmd = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else \"login\"\n    if cmd == \"login\":\n        _, _, user = login()\n        print(json.dumps({\"login\": \"ok\", \"user\": user}))\n        return\n    if cmd in (\"realms\", \"nodes\", \"resources\", \"permissions\"):\n        paths = {\n            \"realms\": \"/api2/json/access/domains\",\n            \"nodes\": \"/api2/json/nodes\",\n            \"resources\": \"/api2/json/cluster/resources\",\n            \"permissions\": \"/api2/json/access/permissions\",\n        }\n        user, _ = load_creds()\n        if cmd == \"permissions\":\n            paths[\"permissions\"] += \"?userid=\" + urllib.parse.quote(user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\")\n        st, d = api(\"GET\", paths[cmd])\n        print(json.dumps({\"status\": st, \"data\": d.get(\"data\")}, indent=1, default=str))\n        return\n    if cmd == \"vmconfig\" and len(sys.argv) >= 4:\n        st, d = api(\"GET\", \"/api2/json/nodes/%s/qemu/%s/config\" % (sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]))\n        print(json.dumps({\"status\": st, \"data\": d.get(\"data\")}, indent=1, default=str))\n        return\n    if cmd == \"vmstatus\" and len(sys.argv) >= 4:\n        st, d = api(\"GET\", \"/api2/json/nodes/%s/qemu/%s/status/current\" % (sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]))\n        print(json.dumps({\"status\": st, \"data\": d.get(\"data\")}, indent=1, default=str))\n        return\n    if cmd == \"raw\" and len(sys.argv) >= 3:\n        method, path = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n        body = sys.argv[4] if len(sys.argv) > 4 else None\n        ticket, csrf, _ = login()\n        st, d = raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body)\n        print(json.dumps({\"status\": st, \"data\": d}, indent=1, default=str))\n        return\n    print(__doc__)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n", "file_type": ".py"}

{"output": "[exitcode=0]\ndef configuration_export():\n    actor = g.actor\n    payload = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}\n    try:\n        action = action_service.normalize_action(payload.get(\"action\") or \"on\")\n        raw_items = payload.get(\"items\") or []\n        validated = []\n        seen = set()\n        for raw in raw_items:\n            # Export validates with 'on' so protected items can be saved without\n            # implying permission; execution-time checks remain authoritative.\n            item = validate_item(raw.get(\"ip\", \"\"), raw.get(\"outlet\"), \"on\")\n            key = (item[\"ip\"], item[\"outlet\"])\n            if key in seen:\n                continue\n            seen.add(key)\n            validated.append({\"ip\": item[\"ip\"], \"outlet\": item[\"outlet\"]})\n\n        markdown = build_markdown(action, validated)\n        filename = \"pdu-master-control-\" + datetime.datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y%m%d-%H%M%S\") + \".md\"\n        return Response(\n            markdown,\n            mimetype=\"text/markdown\",\n            headers={\"Content-Disposition\": f'attachment; filename=\"{filename}\"'},\n        )\n    except Exception as exc:\n        return jsonify({\"error\": str(exc)}), 400\n\n\n@app.post(\"/configuration/upload\")\ndef configuration_upload():\n    actor = g.actor\n    upload = request.files.get(\"file\")\n    if upload is None or not upload.filename:\n        return jsonify({\"error\": \"No Markdown file was uploaded.\"}), 400\n    if not upload.filename.lower().endswith((\".md\", \".markdown\", \".txt\")):\n        return jsonify({\"error\": \"Upload a .md Markdown configuration file.\"}), 400\n\n    raw = upload.read(1024 * 1024 + 1)\n    if len(raw) > 1024 * 1024:\n        return jsonify({\"error\": \"Configuration file is larger than 1 MB.\"}), 400\n\n    try:\n        text = raw.decode(\"utf-8\")\n        action, items = parse_markdown_config(text)\n        # Validate protected outlets against the imported action, but do not\n        # execute. waiting_for_pdu_lock queue for API submissions on a busy PDU\n  - startup reconciliation of any pending self-host reboot\n\nLegacy behaviours are preserved: one gunicorn worker, threads share runtime\nstructures, command subprocess with hard timeout, state re-read after action.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport base64\nimport datetime\nimport json\nimport os\nimport signal\nimport subprocess\nimport threading\nimport time\nimport uuid\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pdu_ssh_direct as pdu_direct\n\nCONFIG_FILE = \"/etc/pdu-control/config.json\"\nSECRETS_FILE = \"/etc/pdu-control/secrets.env\"\nAUDIT_FILE = \"/var/log/pdu-control/audit.log\"\nAUDIT_DB_FILE = \"/var/lib/pdu-control/audit.sqlite3\"\nWORKER_FILE = \"/opt/pdu-control/pdu_worker.py\"\nPENDING_FILE = \"/var/lib/pdu-control/pending_reboot.json\"\nPYTHON_BIN = \"/opt/pdu-control/venv/bin/python\"\nCOMMAND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 60\nMAX_BATCH_ITEMS = 72\nPROTECTED_RECOVERY_ATTEMPTS = 2  # small configurable limit (V3 14.3)\nSELF_HOST_SETTLE_SECONDS = 8  # seconds after cycle confirm before skipping verify\n\nwith open(CONFIG_FILE, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n    CONFIG = json.load(handle)\n\n\ndef load_secrets():\n    values = {}\n    with open(SECRETS_FILE, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n        for raw in handle:\n            line = raw.strip()\n            if not line or \"=\" not in line:\n                continue\n            key, value = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n            values[key] = value.strip()\n    return values", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Sliding 60s window.\"\"\"\n    now = time.time()\n    limit = RATE_GET_PER_MIN if kind == \"get\" else RATE_POST_PER_MIN\n    with _rl_lock:\n        bucket = _RATE_BUCKETS[identity]\n        q = bucket[kind]\n        while q and q[0] <= now - 60:\n            q.popleft()\n        if len(q) >= limit:\n            return False\n        q.append(now)\n        return True\n\n\ndef _rate_limited_response(kind, identity):\n    with _rl_lock:\n        q = _RATE_BUCKETS[identity][kind]\n        oldest = q[0] if q else time.time()\n    retry_after = max(1, int(60 - (time.time() - oldest)))\n    resp = jsonify({\n        \"error\": {\n            \"code\": \"RATE_LIMITED\",\n            \"message\": f\"Rate limit exceeded for {kind.upper()} requests ({retry_after}s window).\",\n        }\n    })\n    resp.status_code = 429\n    resp.headers[\"Retry-After\"] = str(retry_after)\n    return resp\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Authentication helper (Basic over LLDAP)\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"content": "1|# PDU Manager Reproducible Deployment and CI/CD Plan\n2|\n3|**Date:** 2026-09-26  \n4|**Repository:** `https://github.com/startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`  \n5|**Production target:** VM154 on `MIAM-00133` (`https://10.0.20.154/`)  \n6|**Prerequisite:** Complete and merge the live-source capture only after human review.\n7|\n8|## 1. Goal\n9|\n10|Create a deployment system in which the GitHub repository, not an ad-hoc production VM, is the authoritative source for PDU Manager application code and deployment instructions.\n11|\n12|The pipeline must prove that a fresh environment can host the PDU Manager in the same operational shape as VM154 before production deployment is enabled.\n13|\n14|A code change should follow this lifecycle:\n15|\n16|```mermaid\n17|flowchart LR\n18|    A[Feature branch] --> B[CI: lint/test/security/build]\n19|    B --> C[Pull request]\n20|    C --> D[Human review]\n21|    D -->|approved| E[Merge to main]\n22|    E --> F[Build immutable release artifact]\n23|    F --> G[Deploy to staging]\n24|    G --> H[Read-only parity/smoke tests]\n25|    H --> I{Production approval}\n26|    I -->|approved| J[Deploy to VM154]\n27|    J --> K[Health checks]\n28|    K -->|pass| L[Release complete]\n29|    K -->|fail| M[Automatic rollback]\n30|```\n31|\n32|## 2. Safety principle\n33|\n34|CI/CD validation must never prove correctness by cycling real production outlets.\n35|\n36|Production-safe automated checks may:\n37|\n38|- load the web UI;\n39|- authenticate using a dedicated non-privileged validation account where available;\n40|- parse configuration;\n41|- exercise mock PDU adapters;\n42|- run unit/integration tests;\n43|- fetch read-only outlet state if the backend supports a reliably non-actuating path;\n44|- verify service health and logs.\n45|\n46|Automated checks must not:\n47|\n48|- issue ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE commands;\n49|- alter outlet labels/presets/protection settings;\n50|- reboot VM154;\n51|- restart `MIAM-00133`;\n52|- modify PDU firmware/configuration;\n53|- use production administrator credentials in pull-request jobs.\n54|\n55|## 3. Phase 0 - Do not choose a deployment technology before capture\n56|\n57|The current runtime stack must first be discovered from VM154.\n58|\n59|The CI/CD implementation should preserve the existing runtime unless there is a clear reason to change it.\n60|\n61|Examples:\n62|\n63|- If the service is already a normal Python/systemd application, package and deploy it reproducibly as Python/systemd first.\n64|- If it is already Dockerized, preserve Docker unless there is a strong reason not to.\n65|- Do not introduce Kubernetes.\n66|- Do not containerize merely for aesthetic reasons.\n67|\n68|A migration that changes the production runtime model is a significant architecture decision and requires an ADR and human approval.\n69|\n70|## 4. Phase 1 - Define the reproducibility contract\n71|\n72|After capture, document and automate all prerequisites needed by a clean VM:\n73|\n74|- supported OS/version;\n75|- CPU/RAM/disk minimums;\n76|- runtime version;\n77|- dependency installation;\n78|- application user/group;\n79|- application directories;\n80|- persistent data directories;\n81|- configuration locations;\n82|- secret locations;\n83|- TLS termination;\n84|- systemd/container startup;\n85|- logging;\n86|- network ports;\n87|- LLDAP/authentication integration;\n88|- PDU endpoint configuration;\n89|- health check;\n90|- backup/restore expectations.\n91|\n92|The repository must contain a single supported installation path such as one of:\n93|\n94|```text\n95|./deploy/install.sh\n96|./deploy/bootstrap.sh\n97|ansible-playbook deploy/site.yml\n98|# or the stack-appropriate equivalent discovered during capture\n99|```\n100|\n101|Do not leave the clean-install process as undocumented manual shell history.\n102|\n103|## 5. Phase 2 - Separate code, configuration, secrets, and mutable state\n104|\n105|Production deployment must distinguish:\n106|\n107|### Code\n108|Versioned in Git and deployed from an immutable release artifact.\n109|\n110|### Non-secret site configuration\n111|May be versioned if appropriate, for example device names, safe defaults, and mapping schema.\n112|\n113|### Secrets\n114|Never committed. Examples include:\n115|\n116|- PDU login credentials;\n117|- LDAP bind passwords;\n118|- application session secret;\n119|- TLS private key;\n120|- GitHub deployment credentials.\n121|\n122|Store secrets using either:\n123|\n124|1. runner/VM local protected files such as `/etc/pdu-manager/secrets.env`; or\n125|2. GitHub Environment secrets delivered only during an approved deployment.\n126|\n127|Do not print secrets to CI logs.\n128|\n129|### Mutable runtime state\n130|Logs, uploaded configuration, generated state, databases, and similar data must live outside immutable release directories and survive code rollbacks.\n131|\n132|## 6. Phase 3 - Build an isolated staging environment\n133|\n134|Create a disposable staging VM that is independent of VM154.\n135|\n136|Recommended characteristics:\n137|\n138|- same OS family/version as production;\n139|- same application runtime;\n140|- different IP/hostname;\n141|- no ability to actuate production PDU outlets;\n142|- mock PDU backend by default;\n143|- optional read-only production status integration only if technically enforceable.\n144|\n145|The staging environment is the acceptance test for reproducibility.\n146|\n147|### Required staging proof\n148|\n149|Starting from a clean OS image, the deployment automation must be able to:\n150|\n151|", "total_lines": 377, "file_size": 13185, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=151 to continue reading (showing 1-150 of 377 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "151|1. install prerequisites;\n152|2. create application directories/users;\n153|3. install the release artifact;\n154|4. install/enable service definitions;\n155|5. install safe test configuration;\n156|6. start the application;\n157|7. expose the expected HTTPS/UI endpoint;\n158|8. pass health checks;\n159|9. load the major UI views;\n160|10. run mock backend operations without touching real PDUs;\n161|11. survive a normal application-service restart;\n162|12. redeploy the same version idempotently.\n163|\n164|If this cannot be done, production CD is not ready.\n165|\n166|## 7. Phase 4 - CI pipeline\n167|\n168|Use GitHub Actions unless a human approves another CI provider.\n169|\n170|### Pull-request CI\n171|\n172|Every PR should run, as appropriate to the discovered stack:\n173|\n174|1. repository policy/Markdown checks;\n175|2. formatting;\n176|3. lint/static analysis;\n177|4. type checking where applicable;\n178|5. unit tests;\n179|6. integration tests against mocks/fixtures;\n180|7. build/package validation;\n181|8. dependency vulnerability scan;\n182|9. secret scan;\n183|10. configuration-schema validation;\n184|11. deployment-script syntax/static checks;\n185|12. creation of an immutable test artifact.\n186|\n187|No PR CI job should need production PDU credentials.\n188|\n189|### Main-branch CI\n190|\n191|After a human-approved merge:\n192|\n193|1. rerun required checks;\n194|2. build one release artifact identified by commit SHA;\n195|3. generate a manifest containing version, dependencies, and checksums;\n196|4. retain the artifact for staging and production so production receives the exact artifact already tested.\n197|\n198|Do not rebuild different production bits after staging validation.\n199|\n200|## 8. Phase 5 - Runner architecture\n201|\n202|Public GitHub-hosted runners cannot directly reach private `10.0.20.x` addresses.\n203|\n204|The preferred production deployment design is a **dedicated least-privilege self-hosted GitHub Actions runner inside the MARION-IA-USA management network**, not a root runner on VM154 itself.\n205|\n206|Recommended placement:\n207|\n208|- a small dedicated VM/CT on the management network; or\n209|- another explicitly approved management host.\n210|\n211|Avoid placing a general GitHub runner directly on `MIAM-00133` as root unless a human explicitly accepts that security boundary.\n212|\n213|The runner should have only the permissions required to:\n214|\n215|- fetch a previously built release artifact;\n216|- connect to VM154 using a dedicated deployment identity;\n217|- transfer release files;\n218|- invoke the controlled deployment script;\n219|- run health checks;\n220|- invoke rollback.\n221|\n222|It should not have blanket root access to the entire Proxmox cluster or PDU credentials.\n223|\n224|Because runner placement is a significant security/deployment decision, create a **Proposed ADR** and obtain human approval before installing it.\n225|\n226|## 9. Phase 6 - Production release layout\n227|\n228|Unless the captured stack has a better native mechanism, prefer an immutable release layout such as:\n229|\n230|```text\n231|/opt/pdu-manager/\n232|  current -> releases/<commit-sha>/\n233|  releases/\n234|    <commit-sha>/\n235|    <previous-sha>/\n236|/etc/pdu-manager/\n237|  config.*\n238|  secrets.env\n239|/var/lib/pdu-manager/\n240|/var/log/pdu-manager/\n241|```\n242|\n243|The deployment procedure should:\n244|\n245|1. acquire a deployment lock;\n246|2. verify the release artifact checksum;\n247|3. back up mutable configuration/state required for recovery;\n248|4. install into a new release directory;\n249|5. install dependencies in an isolated versioned environment where appropriate;\n250|6. validate configuration without starting the new release;\n251|7. switch `current` atomically;\n252|8. restart only the PDU Manager application service(s), not VM154;\n253|9. run health/read-only smoke checks;\n254|10. mark success or automatically restore the prior `current` target and restart on failure.\n255|\n256|If the current stack is Docker, adapt this concept to immutable image digests rather than forcing a symlink layout.\n257|\n258|## 10. Phase 7 - Production deployment trigger\n259|\n260|Recommended governance:\n261|\n262|- feature branches: CI only;\n263|- PR: CI only;\n264|- merge to `main`: build artifact and deploy to staging;\n265|- staging success: production deployment becomes eligible;\n266|- production: protected GitHub Environment with explicit human approval;\n267|- after approval: deploy exact staging-tested artifact to VM154.\n268|\n269|This satisfies the user's goal that merged code can be pushed to production while preserving the repository's human-approval control point.\n270|\n271|Do not configure unreviewed pull requests to deploy automatically to production.\n272|\n273|## 11. Phase 8 - Deployment health checks\n274|\n275|A production deployment is successful only if all safe checks pass, including:\n276|\n277|- expected service unit/container is running;\n278|- expected port is listening;\n279|- HTTPS endpoint responds;\n280|- main UI renders;\n281|- static assets load;\n282|- configuration loads without error;\n283|- authentication health is acceptable;\n284|- PDU backend initializes;\n285|- read-only state retrieval works if it can be performed without actuation;\n286|- recent service logs contain no new fatal errors.\n287|\n288|Do not send a power command as a smoke test.\n289|\n290|## 12. Phase 9 - Rollback\n291|\n292|Rollback must be documented and tested in staging before production CD is enabled.\n293|\n294|Rollback should restore:\n295|\n296|- prior code artifact/release;\n297|- compatible application dependency set;\n298|- prior service configuration when deployment changed it;\n299|- prior mutable data only when a schema/config migration requires it.\n300|\n301|The deployment system should retain at least the immediately previous known-good release.\n302|\n303|If a database or persistent state migration is later introduced, every migration must define whether rollback is safe. Irreversible migrations require explicit human approval and a backup.\n304|\n305|## 13. Phase 10 - Parity validation against current VM154\n306|\n307|Before declaring the GitHub deployment equivalent to current production, compare staging against VM154 for:\n308|\n309|- visible outlet inventory;\n310|- outlet names;\n311|- PDU grouping;\n312|- protected/locked outlets;\n313|- presets;\n314|- UI navigation;\n315|- authentication behavior;\n316|- status refresh behavior;\n317|- configuration export/import format;\n318|- logs/recent actions;\n319|- mobile layout where practical;\n320|- KVM-related labels, including `MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console` and `MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM`.\n321|\n322|Use screenshots/config diffs/read-only queries. Do not use production actuation for parity testing.\n323|\n324|Any intentional difference must map to an approved requirement and, when architecturally significant, an ADR.\n325|\n326|## 14. CI/CD acceptance criteria\n327|\n328|Do not call CI/CD complete until:\n329|\n330|- [ ] a clean staging VM can be deployed entirely from repository instructions/automation;\n331|- [ ] all production secrets are external to Git;\n332|- [ ] CI runs on every PR;\n333|- [ ] CI uses no production actuation credentials;\n334|- [ ] a single immutable artifact flows from main build to staging to production;\n335|- [ ] staging deployment is automatic after approved merge, or otherwise consistently automated;\n336|- [ ] production deployment requires the configured human approval gate;\n337|- [ ] production deployment restarts only application services, not the VM;\n338|- [ ] health checks are read-only;\n339|- [ ] rollback has been tested on staging;\n340|- [ ] deployment logs identify commit/release SHA;\n341|- [ ] branch protection prevents direct unreviewed production changes;\n342|- [ ] source and documentation describe the actual deployment accurately.\n343|\n344|## 15. Suggested implementation sequence\n345|\n346|1. Merge the reviewed source-capture PR.\n347|2. Create/approve an ADR for the deployment/runner model.\n348|3. Add deterministic install/bootstrap automation.\n349|4. Add mocks and tests for the PDU backend.\n350|5. Add CI workflow.\n351|6. Build a clean staging VM.\n352|7. Prove clean deployment from source.\n353|8. Add immutable release packaging.\n354|9. Add self-hosted deployment runner with least privilege.\n355|10. Deploy automatically to staging after main merges.\n356|11. Add protected production GitHub Environment.\n357|12. Test rollback in staging.\n358|13. Perform one supervised production deployment to VM154.\n359|14. Confirm parity and logs.\n360|15. Declare VM154 rebuildable from GitHub and retire ad-hoc in-place code editing as the normal maintenance method.\n361|\n362|## 16. Required handoff\n363|\n364|The CI/CD implementation agent must leave a Markdown handoff recording:\n365|\n366|- workflow files added;\n367|- runner placement and permissions;\n368|- deployment identity and secret locations (names/paths only, never values);\n369|- staging VM identity;\n370|- exact install/deploy/rollback commands;\n371|- test matrix;\n372|- latest staging deployment result;\n373|- latest production deployment result if performed;\n374|- rollback test result;\n375|- known limitations;\n376|- ADR/TDR state;\n377|- remaining future work.\n378|", "total_lines": 377, "file_size": 13185, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|# LLM Manager — VM Reproducibility + GitHub CI/CD Production Deployment Plan\n2|\n3|**Date:** 2026-09-25  \n4|**Repository:** `startupteams/llm-manager-project-framework`  \n5|**Current production:** VM114 on MARION-IA-USA / MIAM-00135\n6|\n7|## 0. Goal\n8|\n9|Build a safe path from reviewed GitHub source to production:\n10|\n11|```text\n12|branch/PR\n13|  -> GitHub CI\n14|  -> merge to main\n15|  -> immutable release artifact\n16|  -> automatic staging deployment\n17|  -> staging acceptance\n18|  -> human-approved production promotion\n19|  -> VM114\n20|  -> health checks\n21|  -> automatic application rollback on failure\n22|```\n23|\n24|The first gate is **reproducibility**:\n25|\n26|> A clean Ubuntu 24.04 VM must be deployable from this repository plus documented external dependencies/secrets and behave like the current VM114.\n27|\n28|If that fails, fix the source capture or deployment method before enabling production CD.\n29|\n30|## 1. Current-state findings\n31|\n32|The repo already captures:\n33|\n34|```text\n35|service/app/\n36|service/collectors/\n37|service/control/\n38|ops/systemd/\n39|ops/nginx/\n40|ops/litellm/\n41|config/examples/\n42|db/schema/schema.sql\n43|```\n44|\n45|Current deployment maps these to `/opt/llm-manager`, `/etc/systemd/system`, `/etc/nginx`, and `/etc/llm-manager`.\n46|\n47|Current production includes FastAPI/uvicorn, LiteLLM, recovery, GPU-power collector, Emporia collector, nginx, PostgreSQL, LLDAP, Proxmox control, model endpoints, and a documented `/agents/` dependency.\n48|\n49|Important gaps before CI/CD:\n50|\n51|1. No `.github/workflows/` exists.\n52|2. No automated clean-VM installer/bootstrap exists.\n53|3. Systemd units hard-code `/opt/llm-manager/...`.\n54|4. DB changes are manual/additive; no migration runner exists.\n55|5. `litellm_config.yaml` is generated runtime state and must not blindly overwrite production.\n56|6. Current tests are too small to prove hostability.\n57|7. No real staging VM exists.\n58|8. Resolve the documented Python-version inconsistency; the captured runtime file says Python 3.12.3.\n59|9. Determine whether AgentManager on `127.0.0.1:8200` is an external dependency or missing captured code.\n60|10. Recovery/registry synchronization can mutate state and must be handled deliberately during deployment.\n61|\n62|## 2. Recommended production layout\n63|\n64|Do not deploy with `git pull` directly into the live tree.\n65|\n66|Use immutable releases:\n67|\n68|```text\n69|/opt/llm-manager/\n70|├── releases/\n71|│   ├── <git-sha-1>/\n72|│   └── <git-sha-2>/\n73|├── current -> /opt/llm-manager/releases/<active-sha>\n74|└── shared/\n75|```\n76|\n77|Keep mutable/secrets outside releases:\n78|\n79|```text\n80|/etc/llm-manager/\n81|├── secrets/\n82|├── tls/\n83|├── litellm.env\n84|└── litellm_config.yaml\n85|```\n86|\n87|Update systemd to point to `/opt/llm-manager/current/...`.\n88|\n89|Benefits: exact Git-SHA identity, atomic-ish promotion, simple rollback, no untracked production edits.\n90|\n91|## 3. Repo additions\n92|\n93|Add:\n94|\n95|```text\n96|.github/workflows/\n97|  ci.yml\n98|  deploy-staging.yml\n99|  deploy-production.yml\n100|\n101|deploy/\n102|  README.md\n103|  build-release.sh\n104|  bootstrap-vm.sh\n105|  preflight.sh\n106|  deploy-release.sh\n107|  healthcheck.sh\n108|  rollback.sh\n109|  verify-parity.sh\n110|\n111|db/migrations/\n112|  README.md\n113|  <numbered migrations>.sql\n114|\n115|tests/\n116|  unit/\n117|  integration/\n118|  fixtures/\n119|  mocks/\n120|\n121|", "total_lines": 865, "file_size": 20957, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=121 to continue reading (showing 1-120 of 865 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

[Content of 2026-09-26-PDU-MANAGER-SOURCE-CAPTURE-AND-GITHUB-COMMIT-PLAN.md]:
# PDU Manager Live-Service Capture and GitHub Commit Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Target repository:** `https://github.com/startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`  
**Production host:** Proxmox node `MIAM-00133`  
**Production guest:** VM `154`  
**Current web UI:** `https://10.0.20.154/`  
**Execution mode:** Autonomous discovery and capture, with explicit stop gates for secrets, destructive actions, significant architecture changes, and merge/production deployment.

## 1. Mission

Capture the complete, currently running PDU Manager service from VM154 into the GitHub repository so that the repository becomes an understandable, reviewable, reproducible source of truth.

The agent must capture enough information to answer all of the following from GitHub alone:

1. What the PDU Manager is for and why the business operates it.
2. What code actually serves `https://10.0.20.154/`.
3. How the service is started and kept running.
4. What runtime, packages, services, files, configuration, certificates, and external integrations it depends on.
5. Which configuration is safe to commit and which values are secrets or site-local state.
6. How a clean VM can be made to host the same service.
7. How to validate the service without turning any PDU outlet on, off, or rebooting it.
8. Which behavior is implemented today and which behavior is only planned.

The final code capture must be committed on a branch and presented for human review. **Do not merge the branch autonomously.**

## 2. Business reason

The PDU Manager is operational infrastructure for the MARION-IA-USA server environment. It centralizes remote visibility and controlled power operations for rack equipment connected to network-managed PDUs. Capturing the service in GitHub is required because a service that exists only as mutable files on a production VM is difficult to reproduce, review, test, recover, or safely modify.

Moving the source into GitHub provides:

- disaster-recovery capability if VM154 is lost;
- traceable changes to power-control logic and outlet metadata;
- human review before production changes;
- reproducible deployment instead of one-off shell edits;
- automated validation and secret scanning;
- a clear handoff point for AI agents and humans;
- a safe foundation for CI/CD;
- protection against accidental configuration drift between documentation, source, and the running service.

## 3. Known production context to preserve and verify

Treat these as **known environment facts / intended behavior that must be verified against the live VM**, not as permission to invent implementation details.

- The PDU Manager runs on VM154 hosted by Proxmox node `MIAM-00133`.
- The user reaches the service at `https://10.0.20.154/`.
- The environment contains three network-managed PDU endpoints at `10.0.20.151`, `10.0.20.152`, and `10.0.20.153`.
- The UI currently identifies the backend as a direct SSH / PowerAlert style control path; the exact implementation must be verified from code and runtime configuration.
- LLDAP at `10.0.20.101` has been used as the authentication directory for this environment; verify whether and how VM154 currently uses it.
- Current UI capabilities to preserve include outlet state display, outlet naming, individual ON/OFF/REBOOT actions, batch selection/actions, presets, protection/lockout behavior, recent-action logging, configuration save/upload behavior, and a mobile-usable web UI. Verify each capability against the live service before documenting it as implemented.
- Critical devices must be protected from casual shutdown/reboot. In particular, historical design intent protects `MIAM-00133` from dangerous power actions. Verify the current protection model from live configuration/code.
- KVM-related naming has recently been updated. The expected current naming includes:
  - `MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console`
  - `MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM`
  Verify the live configuration and do not reintroduce deprecated KVM labels.

## 4. Non-negotiable safety rules

The capture task is **read-only against production behavior** except for creating temporary capture files and, later, making explicitly reviewed source-control changes in a separate workspace.

The agent MUST NOT during capture or validation:

- send PDU `OFF`, `ON`, `REBOOT`, `CYCLE`, or equivalent actuation commands;
- click production power-control buttons as a test;
- reboot or shut down VM154 or `MIAM-00133`;
- change production outlet names, presets, lockouts, protection flags, credentials, authentication settings, firewall rules, or network configuration;
- rotate secrets;
- edit the running service in place merely to make it easier to capture;
- overwrite production configuration with repository files;
- commit credentials, passwords, tokens, private keys, session secrets, TLS private keys, LLDAP bind credentials, or PDU credentials;
- merge its own pull request.

If any action is required that could alter power state, infrastructure, permissions, secrets, or production data, STOP and request human approval.

## 5. Repository rules

Before making repository changes, read:

1. `README.md`
2. `AGENTS.md`
3. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
6. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
7. `docs/adr/`
8. `docs/tdr/`

Follow the repository's human-approval model.

If no issue/ticket already exists for this work, create one before committing so branch and commit naming can comply with repository policy.

Suggested issue title:

`Capture live PDU Manager source and establish reproducible deployment baseline`

Suggested branch after the issue number is known:

`chore/<ISSUE>-capture-live-pdu-manager`

Suggested commit subject after the issue number is known:

`chore(capture): capture live PDU manager baseline (#<ISSUE>)`

Do not commit directly to `main`.

## 6. Phase A - Establish a production inventory without changing production

From the `MIAM-00133` root shell, begin by proving VM154 exists and is running:

```bash
qm status 154
qm config 154
```

Use the QEMU guest agent where available:

```bash
qm guest exec 154 -- bash -lc 'hostname; date; uname -a; cat /etc/os-release'
```

Capture the output into the work log.

### 6.1 Discover the serving process

Inside VM154, gather:

```bash
systemctl list-units --type=service --all --no-pager
systemctl list-unit-files --type=service --no-pager
ps auxww
ss -lntup
```

Identify the process listening on TCP 443 and any internal application port. For each candidate process, capture:

- PID;
- command line;
- executable path;
- working directory;
- user/group;
- parent process;
- systemd unit or container name;
- environment-file references;
- restart policy;
- log destination.

Useful read-only commands include:

```bash
readlink -f /proc/<PID>/exe
readlink -f /proc/<PID>/cwd
tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/<PID>/cmdline
systemctl status <unit> --no-pager
systemctl cat <unit>
systemctl show <unit>
```

Do not copy `/proc/<PID>/environ` into the repository. It may contain secrets.

### 6.2 Determine the runtime model

Explicitly determine whether the live service uses any of the following:

- Python / Flask / FastAPI / Gunicorn / Uvicorn;
- Node.js;
- Docker / Docker Compose;
- nginx / Apache / Caddy / another reverse proxy;
- a standalone HTTPS development server;
- SQLite / JSON / YAML / flat-file configuration;
- systemd only;
- cron or systemd timers;
- custom shell wrappers.

Record facts only. Do not migrate to a different runtime during capture.

### 6.3 Find live files, not backups

The earlier KVM-label search revealed many historical copies under `/root`, including backup directories and bootstrap scripts. That is evidence that broad string searches are not sufficient to identify the live application.

The agent must derive the live code path from the running process/service/container first. Then inspect only that live path and its declared dependencies.

For every discovered file/directory, classify it as one of:

- **SOURCE** - application code that belongs in Git;
- **DEPLOYMENT** - service units, reverse-proxy config, installer/bootstrap logic;
- **SAFE CONFIG** - non-secret defaults or site mapping appropriate for Git;
- **SECRET CONFIG** - credentials/keys/tokens; never commit;
- **PERSISTENT STATE** - logs, runtime state, mutable uploaded configuration, databases;
- **GENERATED** - caches, bytecode, build output;
- **BACKUP/LEGACY** - historical copies not used by the live service;
- **UNKNOWN** - requires human review.

### 6.4 Capture service/dependency metadata

Capture, when applicable:

- systemd unit files and drop-ins;
- reverse-proxy configuration;
- application source;
- static assets/templates;
- dependency manifests (`requirements.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, lockfiles, etc.);
- exact runtime versions;
- package/service prerequisites;
- file ownership and permissions needed to run;
- listener ports;
- application health behavior;
- log locations;
- persistent data/config locations;
- TLS termination location;
- authentication integration;
- PDU integration points;
- startup/restart commands.

If dependency manifests do not exist, use the live environment as evidence to create a minimal reproducible manifest, but label it as reconstructed and validate it later on a clean staging VM.

## 7. Phase B - Create a clean capture workspace

Create a workspace **outside the production application directory**. Prefer an agent workstation or a temporary directory on `MIAM-00133`, for example:

```text
/root/pdu-manager-capture-<timestamp>/
```

Do not edit live files.

Transfer only the classified SOURCE, DEPLOYMENT, and SAFE CONFIG files into this workspace.

If SSH/SCP to VM154 is available, use a read-only copy path. If not, use a temporary tar archive inside VM154 and transfer it through an available safe mechanism. Avoid printing binary archives or secrets into terminal history when a direct file-copy mechanism is available.

Generate a capture manifest containing:

- source path on VM154;
- destination path in the repository;
- SHA-256 checksum;
- classification;
- whether the file was changed/sanitized before commit;
- reason for exclusion if not committed.

Save the manifest as `docs/CAPTURE_MANIFEST.md` in the branch.

## 8. Phase C - Secret and sensitive-data review

Before `git add`, inspect all captured files for:

- passwords;
- usernames paired with passwords;
- SSH private keys;
- TLS private keys;
- API tokens;
- cookies/session secrets;
- LDAP bind passwords;
- PDU credentials;
- GitHub credentials;
- embedded Basic Auth URLs;
- `.env` files;
- shell history;
- certificates containing private keys;
- database dumps or logs containing sensitive material.

At minimum run a repository secret scanner such as `gitleaks` if available. Also perform targeted text searches.

For every secret found:

1. Leave the production value unchanged on VM154 during capture.
2. Remove the secret from the Git copy.
3. Replace it with environment/config indirection.
4. Add a safe example such as `.env.example` or `config.example.*` containing placeholder values only.
5. Add the real secret-bearing path to `.gitignore` if needed.
6. Document where production will obtain the secret in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` without revealing the value.
7. If a secret was already committed at any point, STOP and request human assistance for rotation/history remediation.

## 9. Phase D - Establish an understandable repository layout

Preserve the application's natural structure when possible. Do not perform a speculative rewrite during capture.

A likely repository shape after discovery may resemble:

```text
/
  README.md
  AGENTS.md
  REQUIREMENTS.md
  FUTURE_WORK.md
  SPRINT.md
  app/ or src/                 # live application source, exact name chosen from discovered stack
  tests/                       # only real tests; do not create fake passing tests
  deploy/
    systemd/                   # captured/reconstructed unit files
    proxy/                     # reverse proxy config if applicable
    scripts/                   # install/deploy/healthcheck scripts
    examples/                  # safe config examples
  docs/
    ARCHITECTURE.md
    OPERATIONS.md
    DEPLOYMENT.md
    CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md
    CAPTURE_MANIFEST.md
    adr/
    tdr/
```

Do not force this layout if the live application already has a clear conventional structure. The objective is reproducibility, not cosmetic reorganization.

## 10. Phase E - Document the observed implementation

Update the project documentation package supplied with this plan using facts discovered from VM154.

Every statement about implementation should be marked one of:

- **Implemented and verified** - directly observed in live code/runtime;
- **Implemented but not yet fully validated** - present but not safely exercised;
- **Required target** - approved behavior that the captured code must satisfy;
- **Future work** - explicitly not current scope;
- **Unknown / requires human confirmation** - evidence is insufficient.

Do not claim a power-control action was validated by actuating production hardware.

## 11. Phase F - Reproduce the service without touching production

Before calling the capture complete, prove the repository contains enough information to recreate the application in a clean environment.

Preferred validation order:

1. static dependency/install validation;
2. application start with mock or disabled PDU backend;
3. HTTPS/web UI load;
4. configuration parsing;
5. authentication integration in a non-production mode where practical;
6. read-only status path using mocks or a read-only adapter;
7. no-actuation smoke tests.

Use a disposable staging VM/container rather than VM154 for destructive setup testing.

If a clean deployment cannot be achieved from the repository, do **not** hide the gap. Record every missing dependency/manual step in `docs/CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md` and continue improving the captured source until a clean deployment is possible or a human decides to accept temporary technical debt.

## 12. Phase G - Git commit and pull request

Before commit:

```bash
git status
git diff --check
```

Run every project validation command that now exists. Record exact results.

Commit only after:

- live source path is proven from runtime evidence;
- secret scan passes;
- historical backups are excluded;
- generated files/logs are excluded;
- documentation reflects the actual captured stack;
- a clean/staging start has been attempted and results recorded.

Push the feature branch to:

`startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`

Open a pull request using the repository template.

The PR must explicitly state:

- live service paths discovered;
- runtime stack discovered;
- systemd/container/proxy units discovered;
- files captured;
- files intentionally excluded;
- secret remediation performed;
- clean deployment test result;
- known limitations;
- requirements covered;
- ADR/TDR changes;
- exact validation performed;
- whether production VM154 was changed (**expected: no functional production change during capture**).

**STOP after PR creation. A human must approve the merge.**

## 13. Completion criteria

The capture task is complete only when all of the following are true:

- [ ] The exact process serving `https://10.0.20.154/` has been identified.
- [ ] The exact live application directory has been identified from runtime evidence.
- [ ] Source code has been copied from the live deployment without relying on old backup folders.
- [ ] Runtime/service/proxy/dependency definitions are in Git or documented reproducibly.
- [ ] Secrets are excluded and externalized.
- [ ] The repository documents business purpose, requirements, architecture, operations, deployment, limitations, and future work.
- [ ] A clean/staging deployment has been attempted from source.
- [ ] Validation results are recorded honestly.
- [ ] No production power actuation occurred during capture/validation.
- [ ] No production infrastructure reboot was performed for capture.
- [ ] The work is committed on a branch and pushed.
- [ ] A pull request exists.
- [ ] The agent has NOT merged its own PR.

## 14. Required agent handoff

At completion, write `docs/handoffs/<date>-source-capture-handoff.md` or provide an equivalent PR handoff containing:

1. issue and branch;
2. commit SHA(s);
3. PR URL;
4. live source path;
5. service unit/process name;
6. runtime and dependency versions;
7. external dependencies;
8. secret/config strategy;
9. staging reproduction result;
10. validation commands/results;
11. risks and limitations;
12. ADR/TDR status;
13. future work discovered;
14. exact next action for the human reviewer.
